BGP-4 base protocol

RFC 4271 - A Border Gateway Protocol 4 (BGP-4)

Every requirement listed for RFC 4271, the tests linked to it, and their recorded reviews. This summary is included in the automatic compliance check, ./le rfc check.

Overview

Positive

what Ze has

Tested both ways118of 133 mandatory requirements

a positive test checks that Ze does what the requirement demands and a negative one checks that it refuses what the requirement forbids

One polarity plus reason2of 133 mandatory requirements

the requirement admits no counter-case, so one polarity plus a recorded reason supplies the complete test coverage required

One polarity, unexcused0of 133 mandatory requirements

one direction is tested, the other is neither tested nor excused, and nothing states which

Tests shown to detect a fault304304 of 518 tests linked to requirements, 0 without a fault test and 29 with outdated fault tests

each test was observed to fail when the code it checks was deliberately broken. The test, its stated claim, and the relevant code are checked against the saved record for changes; the deliberate fault is not run again. Having both positive and negative tests verifies a requirement only after both tests have been shown to detect a fault

Negative

what Ze owes

Partial test coverage3of 133 mandatory requirements

scoped tests exist; the remaining obligation is unmet or unverified, with zero whole-requirement credit

Declared gap or no test10of 133 mandatory requirements

the requirements the summary declares a gap for, which say what Ze does not do yet, plus the ones no test carries and no annotation excuses. The at-a-glance table lists them apart: declared gaps (at every level), and mandatory requirements with no test and no annotation

Audit verdicts103of 133 mandatory requirements reviewed

5 weak, wrong or unimplemented, 37 no longer current. Each is named below under its own requirement id

Neutral

measures that are neither good news nor bad

Out of scope0of 133 mandatory requirements

an obligation that does not bind Ze, counted once its evidence passes. A {not-applicable} annotation says it never bound; a {feature-declined} annotation says its condition is an optional feature Ze does not offer, and quotes the RFC sentence that makes it optional. An annotation whose evidence fails is not counted here: the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. This classification records applicability and forms part of the total accounted for by the cards on this page

Not applicable0of 133 mandatory requirements

a {not-applicable} annotation says the obligation does not bind Ze. No test is owed only once its evidence passes; one whose evidence fails stays owed, and the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. It remains in the total that these cards add up to

Met below Ze0of 133 mandatory requirements

a {lower-layer} annotation says a layer under Ze performs the behavior, on state Ze installs into that layer, and names the producer that installs it. The obligation binds Ze and stays in the MUST bar, outside Ze verified: it is OS verified once a tagged test checks that layer's behavior on the state Ze installs, with a recorded break, and an OS testing gap until then

Optional feature declined0of 133 mandatory requirements

a {feature-declined} annotation says the obligation is conditional on a feature the RFC makes optional and Ze does not offer, and it quotes the sentence that makes it optional. The condition is false, so nothing is owed once that quote passes its evidence check; one whose quote fails stays owed, and the figures count it as exclusion unchecked. It remains in the total that these cards add up to

Linux cannot do this0of 133 mandatory requirements

Linux cannot provide the primitive this requirement needs. The obligation still binds Ze and stays unmet, but as a limit of the kernel, not of Ze's code: once its limit entry is verified, the row is not owed (OS blocked) and is left out of the MUST bar; one whose entry fails stays owed, and the figures count it as not built. It remains in the total that these cards add up to and earns no test verification credit

Totals

what the cards above are counted against

Mandatory requirements (MUST level)133of 167 requirements this summary lists, at all levels

The RFC's MUST, MUST NOT, SHALL, SHALL NOT and REQUIRED requirements included in the automatic checks: the compliance check (./le rfc check) holds Ze to each of them. This is the total being assessed; the counts above show the recorded status of those requirements.

The 9 counts marked as a part above are the whole of the 133 mandatory requirements: they add up to 133. Tests shown to detect a fault counts individual tests rather than requirements, so its count is separate.

How to read the colors

A color names what the measure MEANS, not how well Ze scores on it. Green is a good outcome at any value, red is a bad one, and neither a population nor a scope count is an outcome, so both take no color. The number under the label is what says how far Ze has got. An outlined card with no colored edge, under Totals, is a total the other cards are counted against, not a measure.

CardTone hereWhy that color
Out of scope neutral no color: an obligation that never bound Ze is neither an achievement nor a failure, and counting it either way would be a claim
Tested both ways ok green at every value: a test pair is the outcome this gate exists to produce, and the count under the label is what says how far Ze has got
One polarity plus reason ok green at every value: where no counter-case exists, one polarity IS the complete answer, and a recorded reason is what the gate demands beside it
One polarity, unexcused ok green at zero, RED above it: one test direction is missing without a recorded reason
Partial test coverage bad green at zero, RED above it: testing one clause does not verify the whole requirement
Declared gap or no test bad green at zero, RED above it: a binding obligation that nothing shows Ze meets is owed, whether or not a reason is stated
Not applicable neutral no color: an obligation that never bound Ze is neither an achievement nor a failure, and counting it either way would be a claim
Met below Ze neutral no color: an obligation met below Ze is neither a test Ze wrote nor work Ze owes, and the two green counts above are what says how much Ze's own tests verify
Optional feature declined neutral no color: an obligation whose condition Ze never meets is neither an achievement nor a failure. The absent FEATURE is disclosed on the RFC's own status row, as an implementation gap a later scope decision can revisit
Linux cannot do this neutral neutral at every value: an evidenced platform limit is neither test verification nor a scope exemption
Tests shown to detect a fault ok green at every value: these tests have been shown to detect a deliberate fault. This card counts individual tests separately, so its total cannot be added to the requirement counts above
Audit verdicts bad RED on the first weak, wrong or unimplemented verdict, amber while a verdict is no longer current or a mandatory requirement is unreviewed, green when every one is judged sound and current
Mandatory requirements (MUST level) outlined outlined, with no colored edge: it is the total the other cards are counted against, not a measure, and a larger one is neither good news nor bad

Automatic checks

Included in automatic checks: A Border Gateway Protocol 4 (BGP-4): Section 5.1.4 requires a local-configuration mechanism that removes MULTI_EXIT_DISC from a route. Ze exposes the full obligation as `modify NAME { del { med; } }` on an import chain, before Decision Process phases 1 and 2.

The MUST bar shows mandatory requirements (MUST, MUST NOT, REQUIRED, SHALL and SHALL NOT) that Ze and its operating system must meet. It includes only RFCs whose requirement lists have been checked against the original text. Ze verified means a test has been shown to fail when the required behavior is deliberately broken. Behavior provided by the operating system or a library is OS verified when a test checks it using the settings Ze installs and has also been shown to detect a deliberate fault; until then, it is an OS testing gap. Tests awaiting verification counts requirements a tagged test covers that no deliberate fault has yet been shown to break and that nothing records as unmet. Exclusion unchecked counts requirements claimed not to apply to Ze whose evidence is still to be checked. Not assessed counts requirements no tagged test covers and that nothing records as unmet, so nothing yet says whether Ze meets them. Not built counts requirements the RFC summary or a test review says Ze does not meet, in full or in part. The overview bar adds optional requirements (MAY, NOT RECOMMENDED, OPTIONAL, RECOMMENDED, SHOULD and SHOULD NOT) and requirements that do not apply to Ze. An RFC marked requirements not reviewed is excluded from these figures because its requirement list has not been checked against the original text. Counts are loaded from the RFC data files.

{single-polarity}: the obligation is to ACCEPT a message shape, so there is no non-conformant input to reject -- every UPDATE of this form must be processed, and a negative case would have to assert the absence of an error, which proves nothing (ai/rules/testing.md). The consequence the same paragraph asks for, treating the UPDATE as though WITHDRAWN did not contain the prefix, is RFC4271-4.3-7 and is proven by the same test

{partial}: tested "make any NLRI of that route more specific"; gap "advertising this route to other BGP speakers"; legacy IPv4 relay controls preserve exact NLRI during attribute rebuilds and retain independently received more-specific routes. A recorded admission bypass proves the negative raw-export /24-to-/25 check on ordinary and native route-server sockets with the answer marker retained or stripped: all six cases pass clean, then all four marked cases emit forbidden /25 output while both unmarked controls pass. These records do not prove MP NLRI, all native families, stored-route replay or prior-advertisement withdrawal. The restricted boundary is internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_atomic_aggregate.go::atomicAggregatePolicyAllowed; the stronger product restriction is documented in docs/architecture/bgp/egress-attribute-rules.md

Partial test coverage: Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. Independent scoped rejudge after rereading RFC 4271 Sections 5.1.6 and 9.1.4, the current partial annotation, all three tagged units, their socket relay helpers, runLibraryChain and both producing boundaries. Section 5.1.6 binds the route as received with ATOMIC_AGGREGATE even when export policy strips the answer marker; Section 9.1.4 forbids deaggregation. Tested is "make any NLRI of that route more specific"; Gap is "advertising this route to other BGP speakers"; Producer is internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_atomic_aggregate.go::atomicAggregatePolicyAllowed. The release restriction accepts only original native identities and a retained marker for a marked input that still announces; its equality rule is deliberately stronger than the RFC containment obligation. The legacy positive unit requires exact received /17 and /24 NLRI, zero-length ATOMIC_AGGREGATE and changed MED after an attribute-policy rebuild on ordinary and native route-server socket rails. The independent-specific control requires exact separately received unmarked /25 and /32 output on both rails, excluding a blanket prefix-length clamp. Their existing builder mutant covers remain distinct and do not by themselves prove an independently observed mutant failure on each relay rail. The added negative unit sends a valid received /24 through raw export-policy /25 replacement, with the output marker retained and stripped on each rail; its two unmarked controls require exact /25 output and no withdrawals, while its four marked cases forbid covered more-specific socket output and permit safe suppression or preservation. Direct read of job-atomic-rfc-rewrite-record-f3037485.log confirms all six clean cases pass, then all four marked cases fail on forbidden decoded /25 socket output under the recorded admission bypass while both unmarked controls still pass. Canonical discrimination retains these three distinct covers; this rejudge creates no new mutant observation. These tags and records establish only the declared legacy IPv4 scope. They do not prove MP NLRI, every native family, stored-route replay, prior-advertisement withdrawal or broad ownership/recovery ordering. Those advertising paths remain unmet or unproven for this whole requirement. Verdict stays partial and earns zero whole-requirement credit.

Partial test coverage: Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 6.2 says: All errors detected while processing the OPEN message MUST be indicated by sending the NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error. Tested is "NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error"; Gap is "All errors detected"; Producer is internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_handlers.go::handleOpen. The ordinary and pending matrices present three standard/RFC9072 outer-length failures with legal message-header lengths. Both now reach Session.admitOpen, UnpackOpen and rejectOpenCapabilityError; they require exact NOTIFICATION 2/0, ordinary Idle or retained incumbent OpenConfirm, and cleared pending admission. The pending fixture first reads its independent connection's local OPEN before sending malformed input, preserving the refusal assertions. Exact-length ordinary controls require KEEPALIVE and OpenConfirm; the valid pending collision requires Cease 6/7 and an unchanged incumbent. These controls distinguish framing refusal from unconditional OPEN or collision refusal. The handleOpen extraction delegates decoding and validation without changing this tested obligation. The matrices do not enumerate every OPEN validation class, assert TCP EOF independently, or prove universal incoming admission; this remains partial with zero whole-requirement credit.

RFC4271-5.1.2-3When a given BGP speaker advertises the route to an external peer, the advertising speaker updates the AS_PATH attribute as follows: 1) if the first path segment of the AS_PATH is of type AS_SEQUENCE, the local system prepends its own AS number as the last element of the sequence (put it in the leftmost position with respect to the position of octets in the protocol message). If the act of prepending will cause an overflow in the AS_PATH segment (i.e., more than 255 ASes), it SHOULD prepend a new segment of type AS_SEQUENCE and prepend its own AS number to this new segment. 2) if the first path segment of the AS_PATH is of type AS_SET, the local system prepends a new path segment of type AS_SEQUENCE to the AS_PATH, including its own AS number in that segment. 3) if the AS_PATH is empty, the local system creates a path segment of type AS_SEQUENCE, places its own AS into that segment, and places that segment into the AS_PATH. (§5.1.2)

RFC4271-9.2-3If a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out, the previously advertised route in that Adj-RIB-Out MUST be withdrawn from service by means of an UPDATE message (§9.1.3)
MUST 9.1.3
positiveunit/verifyTestRFC4271ExportExclusionWithdrawsPreviouslyAdvertisedRoute
negativeunit/verifyTestRFC4271AcceptedGenerationRemainsAnnounced

{partial}: tested "previously advertised route"; gap "a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out"; live general and route-server relays now withdraw the exact previously advertised IPv4 prefix after a newer received generation is rejected by export policy; accepted-generation controls carry the changed MED without withdrawal. Their shared-chain mutation can stop at the first general-rail failure, so it is not a separate observed route-server mutant red. Ordinary selected-route dissemination and its export-denial lifecycle remain unimplemented at internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::publishBestChanges; the ordinary selected-route probes remain red under spec-bgp-best-route-advertised-to-peers, and refresh or stale replay has separate unresolved scope under spec-bgp-refresh-reapplies-export-policy

Partial test coverage: Tests and fault test records cover only the part marked Tested; they do not verify the whole requirement. partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 9.1.3 says: If a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out, the previously advertised route in that Adj-RIB-Out MUST be withdrawn from service by means of an UPDATE message (see 9.2). Tested is "previously advertised route"; Gap is "a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out"; Producer is internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::publishBestChanges. Both relay matrices first assert the actual announcement, then either an exact bare withdrawal after export rejection of the newer received generation, or an accepted announcement carrying MED 77 and no withdrawal. The positive's shared-chain mutation may fail on the first general rail before the native route-server arm runs, so its named-unit red does not establish an independent route-server mutant red. These are manually driven relay proofs, not evidence that an ordinary Loc-RIB selection invokes an advertiser. The ordinary selected-route probes remain red, and publishBestChanges emits selection events rather than supplying that missing advertiser. Refresh and stale reconciliation retain separate unresolved scope in their owning spec. No complete Phase 3 or complete RFC support is claimed.

RFC4271-9.1.2-5If the AS_PATH attribute of a BGP route contains an AS loop, the BGP route should be excluded from the Phase 2 decision function (§9.1.2). Detection scans the full AS path and checks that the local autonomous system number does not appear in it. RFC 4271 writes this keyword in lower case, so the level is a recommendation and not a capitalized RFC 2119 SHOULD. The same paragraph places a speaker configured to accept routes with its own autonomous system number in the AS path outside the scope of the document. That out-of-scope case is what the allow-own-as setting selects, so a non-zero allow-own-as is not a deviation from this line.

Gaps and untested MUSTs

RequirementStateReason
RFC4271-5.1.6-1 A BGP speaker that receives a route with the ATOMIC_AGGREGATE attribute MUST NOT make any NLRI of that route more specific (as defined in 9.1.4) when advertising this route to other BGP speakers. (§5.1.6) {partial}, Partial test coverage tested "make any NLRI of that route more specific"; gap "advertising this route to other BGP speakers"; legacy IPv4 relay controls preserve exact NLRI during attribute rebuilds and retain independently received more-specific routes. A recorded admission bypass proves the negative raw-export /24-to-/25 check on ordinary and native route-server sockets with the answer marker retained or stripped: all six cases pass clean, then all four marked cases emit forbidden /25 output while both unmarked controls pass. These records do not prove MP NLRI, all native families, stored-route replay or prior-advertisement withdrawal. The restricted boundary is internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_atomic_aggregate.go::atomicAggregatePolicyAllowed; the stronger product restriction is documented in docs/architecture/bgp/egress-attribute-rules.md
RFC4271-6.2-3 All errors detected while processing the OPEN message MUST be indicated by sending the NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error. (§6.2) {partial}, Partial test coverage tested "NOTIFICATION message with the Error Code OPEN Message Error"; gap "All errors detected"; standard and RFC 9072 outer OPEN framing failures now produce exact NOTIFICATION 2/0 through the ordinary decoder and pending-connection reader; exact-length OPEN controls advance the ordinary handshake, while a valid pending collision retains Cease 6/7. These matrices do not bound every detected OPEN validation class; internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_handlers.go::handleOpen remains the gap-context for the full all-errors obligation. A nine-octet OPEN body is a message-header length error, not the Section 6.2 framing witness
RFC4271-5.1.7-1 A BGP speaker that performs route aggregation MAY add the AGGREGATOR attribute, which SHALL contain its own AS number and IP address. (§5.1.7) {gap}, no test ze performs no route aggregation, so it forms no AGGREGATOR of its own. The aggregator leaf of a static route (internal/component/bgp/yang/ze-bgp-conf.yang, read in internal/component/bgp/config/bgp_routes.go) sends any AS:IP the operator writes with no check against the local AS or address, so the knob can emit an AGGREGATOR that is not the speaker's own. No owner decision declines aggregation, so this is owed with it
RFC4271-9.1.2.2-2 If an implementation chooses to remove MULTI_EXIT_DISC, then the optional comparison on MULTI_EXIT_DISC, if performed, MUST be performed only among EBGP-learned routes. (§9.1.2.2) {gap}, no test the common egress guard is implemented, but normal BGP selected-route readvertisement has no runnable producer and no discriminating proof. `bgp-rib` records selected Loc-RIB state (internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go:738-790), route-server and route-reflector plugins forward cached UPDATEs instead (internal/component/bgp/plugins/rs/server.go:433-434 and internal/component/bgp/plugins/rr/rr.go:188-195), and BGP-to-BGP redistribution is rejected as same-protocol redistribution (internal/core/redistevents/registry.go:144-146)
RFC4271-9.2-2 A route SHALL NOT be installed in the Adj-Rib-Out unless the destination, and NEXT_HOP described by this route, may be forwarded appropriately by the Routing Table. (§9.1.3) {gap}, no test nothing gates Adj-RIB-Out installation on the destination and NEXT_HOP being forwardable. QueueAnnounce records the route unconditionally (internal/component/bgp/rib/outgoing.go:65-101), and the forwarding rails decide only on filters, family negotiation and the route-reflection rules (internal/component/bgp/reactor/forward_rs.go:295-333)
RFC4271-9.2-3 If a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out, the previously advertised route in that Adj-RIB-Out MUST be withdrawn from service by means of an UPDATE message (§9.1.3) {partial}, Partial test coverage tested "previously advertised route"; gap "a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out"; live general and route-server relays now withdraw the exact previously advertised IPv4 prefix after a newer received generation is rejected by export policy; accepted-generation controls carry the changed MED without withdrawal. Their shared-chain mutation can stop at the first general-rail failure, so it is not a separate observed route-server mutant red. Ordinary selected-route dissemination and its export-denial lifecycle remain unimplemented at internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::publishBestChanges; the ordinary selected-route probes remain red under spec-bgp-best-route-advertised-to-peers, and refresh or stale replay has separate unresolved scope under spec-bgp-refresh-reapplies-export-policy
RFC4271-9.2.1.1-2 Two UPDATE messages sent by a BGP speaker to a peer that advertise feasible routes and/or withdrawal of unfeasible routes to some common set of destinations MUST be separated by at least MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer. (§9.2.1.1) {gap}, no test ze has no MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer, so successive UPDATEs to a common set of destinations are not spaced. RFC4271-9.2.1.1-3 If new routes are selected multiple times while awaiting the expiration of MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer, the last route selected SHALL be advertised at the end of MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer. (§9.2.1.1) {gap}, no test with no MinRouteAdvertisementIntervalTimer there is no expiry at which a last-selected route could be advertised. The timer is absent by design note (internal/component/bgp/fsm/timer.go:39) and no producer buffers a pending best-route advertisement against such a timer; best-path changes are published as they are computed (internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go:832-880)

Test status

A test reads not verified when no record shows it failing after a deliberate fault. Until then, the test has not been shown to detect a fault in the required behavior.

RFC4271-4.1-3

The value of the Length field MUST always be at least 19 and no greater than 4096 (§4.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/bgp/message/rfc4271_test.go::TestRFC4271MessageLengthOutOfBounds, internal/component/bgp/message/rfc4271_test.go::TestRFC4271MessageLengthWithinBounds moved. RFC 4271 Section 4.1: “The value of the Length field MUST always be at least 19 and no greater than 4096, and MAY be further constrained, depending on the message type.” The changed sender test supplies an equivalent all-false per-family ADD-PATH selector. It establishes that 1500 prefixes exceed one 4096-byte UPDATE, encodes every emitted chunk, requires declared Length equal to written octets within 19..4096, and requires every prefix in original order. Receive controls accept 19, 1000 and 4096 and reject 18, 4097 and 65535 without Extended Message, with exact header-error classification. Producer tracing confirms size budgets include the BGP header; the final ordinary writer sizes effective post-policy bytes, consumes split chunks synchronously and refuses unencodable output instead of emitting a truncated frame. Negotiated RFC 8654 extension remains a separate qualification. This judgment is based on assertions and current source, not producer-halt proof alone.

RFC4271-4.3-3

The lower-order four bits of the Attribute Flags octet are unused. They MUST be zero when sent (§4.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently rejudged against RFC 4271 Section 4.3: "The lower-order four bits of the Attribute Flags octet are unused. They MUST be zero when sent and MUST be ignored when received." normalizeTransmittedUpdate clears 0x0F on every complete path-attribute header in the session's private write buffer. The four writer-rail tests assert every flag, attribute code/value and exact NLRI, including unknown optional transitive attributes and Extended Length; the clean-flags control requires byte identity. General and route-server relay tests require zero low bits and retained values/NLRI. The real-daemon CI requires both complete peer frames; explicit next-hop unchanged preserves its literal next-hop subject without changing a flags oracle. The core encoder witness covers selected locally constructed attributes, while the final session writer covers retained/raw bytes. The producer-disabling native breaks are not claimed as selective bit-corruption experiments. This verdict judges the assertions and does not claim a new execution or completion of unrelated attributes/framing requirements.

Independently rejudged against the complete RFC 4271 Section 4.3 sentence: "The lower-order four bits of the Attribute Flags octet are unused. The actual CI observation is receipt and relay, not a separate RIB-installation query; no direct installation claim forms part of this judgment. The explicit Unchanged leaf preserves all original flag/attribute/NLRI expectations. Coarse producer-disabling records are not claimed as selective reserved-bit mutations, and no new runtime result is asserted here.

RFC4271-5-3

Re-judged 2026-10-31 by an independent auditor who authored none of the change. RFC text (rfc4271): "If a path with an unrecognized transitive optional attribute is accepted and passed to other BGP peers, then the unrecognized transitive optional attribute of that path MUST be passed, along with the path, to other BGP peers with the Partial bit in the Attribute Flags octet set to 1." The only edit to test/plugin/rfc4271-partial-unknown-transitive.ci since the last judgement is f8f7628bdf, which adds a bgp-level filter { import { final accept; } export { final accept; } } with no filter refs, because RFC 8212 Section 3 now makes an EBGP direction with no explicit policy reject. No expect, reject, input or requirement-tag line changed. policy.ConcatChains keeps every peer-level ref and takes the final from the most specific configured level, so a peer-level chain still decides where one exists. An accepting final with no refs neither refuses nor rewrites a route: a withheld, stripped or discarded outcome the file asserts can still come only from the producer under test, and a delivered outcome is the eligibility the test was written under before RFC 8212; without the block the positives would fail for the wrong reason. What the unit proves is unchanged. Verdict unchanged: enforced. Earlier judgements are in this file's git history.

Audit verdict: weak (the tests pass over code that does not enforce the requirement), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_next_hop_test.go::TestEgressNextHopIsPeerOwnReadsTheRewrittenAddress, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_next_hop_test.go::TestEgressNextHopIsPeerOwnReadsTheRewrittenAddress#2, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_next_hop_test.go::TestSendUpdateWithholdsOriginatedRouteWithPeerOwnNextHop, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_forward_next_hop_test.go::TestSendUpdateWithholdsOriginatedRouteWithPeerOwnNextHop#2, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_scoped_next_hop_test.go::TestRFC4271ScopedNextHopPreservesThirdParty, internal/component/bgp/reactor/rfc4271_scoped_next_hop_test.go::TestRFC4271ScopedNextHopWithholdsPeerOwn, internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_rfc.go::checkSelfNextHopWithheld, internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_rfc.go::checkSelfNextHopWithheld#2, test/plugin/originated-nexthop-peer-own.ci, test/plugin/originated-nexthop-peer-own.ci#2, test/plugin/raw-originated-nexthop-peer-own.ci, test/plugin/raw-originated-nexthop-peer-own.ci#2 moved. Independent current-source rejudgment of the whole RFC4271 Section 5.1.3 sentence: A route originated by a BGP speaker SHALL NOT be advertised to a peer using an address of that peer as NEXT_HOP. The prior unimplemented finding described a real raw-originated leak, but its raw producer description is obsolete. Current writeOriginatedRawUpdate asks aigpNextHop(body).has(s.settings.Address); writeOriginatedPeerOwnUpdate refuses the offending active announcement while splitting mixed input to preserve original withdrawals and permitted families. Its retained input protects synchronous callbacks that reuse the session write buffer. The registered diagnostic identifies the refusal and corrective action. Q1: the raw unit's exact absence of prohibited NLRI and literal useful prefix plus third-party NEXT_HOP, mixed-family and borrowed-input controls, ordinary typed writer, queued-default, effective-rewrite, relay, functional text-update and FRR decode oracles judge the advertising prohibition. Q2: main observed the original raw leak RED, then the primary raw unit, mixed-family unit and generic borrowed-input unit PASS under race in job-next-hop-raw-guard-green-239822e1.log; all named subcases passed. The actual raw daemon draft also passed both command forms with same-session useful preceding/following announcements, literal third-party NEXT_HOP and owed withdrawals in job-next-hop-raw-daemon-green-a4275b22.log. These are scoped observations, not current selective mutant records, promoted functional evidence or completed FRR acceptance. Q3: the new raw unit uses matching actual ASN4/family negotiation, a valid local AS path and explicit accepting export final; its third-party twin changes only NEXT_HOP. The existing three general/native relay tests still violate their post-ingress fixture boundary: nhForward and nhForwardRS put nhPayload's ASN2 bytes/context into a cache that requires canonical four-octet AS-bearing input. TranscodeASPath only narrows and rejects this synthetic widening. Their owner-withdrawal assertions therefore fail for the wrong boundary condition; the canonical-byte fixture proposal remains pending. Q4: one unconditional prohibition covers typed and raw originated routes; no truthful source-clause partial partition exists. The identified raw admission behavior is now implemented and has scoped red-to-green evidence, so retaining its old source-gap annotation would be false. Whole-row enforcement remains unproven while the current relay fixture defects, current selective guard proofs, daemon promotion/stress, remaining raw controls and interop validation are outstanding. Verdict weak describes the currently confounded tagged population, retains existing meaningful typed/relay and new raw evidence, and claims no whole-requirement credit. No new current-source RFC4271-5.1.3-1 product defect is asserted by this disposition.

RFC4271-8.2.1-3

For each incoming connection, a state machine MUST be instantiated (§8.2.1)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independently rejudged against RFC 4271 Sections 8, 8.2.1, 8.2.1.2 and 6.7, the relevant RFC 4486 Section 4 refusal distinction, RFC 6286 collision updates, RFC 6608 unexpected-message handling, RFC 7606 UPDATE handling and the RFC Editor inline-errata rendition. Section 8 states: "The data structures and FSM described in this document are conceptual and do not have to be implemented precisely as described here, as long as the implementations support the described functionality and they exhibit the same externally visible behavior." Section 8.2.1's incoming requirement therefore binds connection behavior, not the presence of a particular Session/FSM object. The positive listener unit receives Ze's OPEN on the admitted incoming TCP connection before sending its remote OPEN and completes that connection's OPEN/KEEPALIVE exchange while the outgoing connection has received no remote OPEN. The separate negative reaches EOF on a held incoming connection, observes exactly one retry increment and released pending ownership, then establishes the original held connection on its original TCP socket. These are useful success and failure-isolation outcomes, not object-count assertions alone. Both current native records replace newIncomingSession's isolated exchange with the incumbent: main observed each clean named unit PASS and each broken named unit FAIL, respectively missing incoming OPEN and missing the candidate's held DelayOpen state. The input isolates this per-connection ownership obligation and the two polarities are separate functions. Reachable producers preserve the incoming Session, reader and timer lifecycle through collision selection; local capacity refusal before that exchange is permitted by Section 6.7 and RFC 4486 Section 4 and does not owe an otherwise unused FSM object. Current capacity refusal sends 6/5; an actual OPEN-based collision loser retains 6/7. Additional untagged capacity controls observed by main exercise refusal/EOF, the same admitted connection's true collision outcome and useful incumbent traffic. They support the refusal interpretation without becoming extra tagged whole-row proof. This judgment covers the stated incoming handshake and failure ownership obligation; it does not claim every plugin callback, every BGP requirement or live interoperability has been proven. A separately identified provisional role-callback publication defect remains owned by its correction and independent closure work. Earlier behavioral reclassification rationale retained: The earlier partial finding inferred a universal conformance gap solely because refused additional TCP sockets did not construct Session/FSM objects. That inference contradicted RFC 4271 Section 8's explicit conceptual-structure provision and ignored Section 6.7 and RFC 4486 Section 4's permitted local refusal behavior. Re-read the primary text, actual admission/handshake/retirement producers, both complete tagged bodies and renewed native records. The existing bodies already discriminate the required per-connection handshake and isolated failure behavior; correcting their claim wording and withdrawing the structural-only gap warrants enforced rather than constructing unused objects or requiring unlimited incoming admission. All existing assertions remain intact. Final closure rejudgment: independently reread RFC 4271 Sections 8, 8.2.1, 8.2.1.2, 6.7 and 9, all three current tagged functions and their assertion-producing helpers, and the complete current incoming factory/read/adopt paths. The existing positive incoming handshake and negative EOF/failure-isolation outcomes remain distinct behavioral proofs. The added positive TestIncomingBeforeOpenUpdateCannotPublishIncumbentWithdrawal uses two actual listener sockets and the real delivery worker in both receive modes. With the new connection held in Active before OPEN, its otherwise valid withdrawal must reach neither received-UPDATE callback nor delivery queue; an Established sibling retains its modeled route through a legal FIFO fence, then delivers its own legitimate withdrawal exactly once. The body expressly models route presence from worker delivery and makes no actual Adj-RIB-In or Loc-RIB claim. The shared updateIsUnexpected producer now admits UPDATEs only in Established, matching Section 9, before either rail publishes semantics; add that helper to the code fingerprint map. The private decoded OPEN pair likewise remains connection-owned until validated selection, while raw wire capture and physical OPEN counters retain their original boundaries. These source corrections strengthen externally visible connection isolation without reinstating a structural object-count obligation. Earlier historical wording about a separately owned publication defect describes the prior checkpoint; the current producer isolation is the subject of this final re-read. Native discrimination receipts are recorded separately; this source judgment does not claim tests or interop were executed by the auditor.

Re-read 2026-10-01 (c8 judge): the only change under this verdict is the deleted RFC4271-8.2.2-18 tag line in TestRFC4271OpenSentWellFormedOpenKeepsTheConnection's doc comment (8.2.2-18 retired into 8.2.2-8); the unit's body and assertions are unchanged. D-8 fixed: CloseWithNotification now sets ErrCollisionDump and signals errChan, so the Run loop no longer outlives the connection. Peer level in OpenSent: exactly one 6/7, EOF, three timers stopped, session dropped, counter exactly 1; shared negative keeps counter 3. CloseWithNotification break re-recorded after the fix, observed red. The ConnectRetryTimer clause holds by construction: fsm Timers.StartConnectRetryTimer has no non-test caller, so the peer-level 'not running' assertion guards a future arming, and the HEAD rfc4271_test.go unit arms it and proves StopAll stops it.

RFC4271-3.1-2

The next hop for each of these routes MUST be resolvable via the local BGP speaker's Routing Table. (§3.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 3.2 requires every Loc-RIB route's next hop to be resolvable through the local Routing Table. Independently read the six tagged CI covers, both real TCP ingress scenarios, their selection/Loc-RIB/kernel observers and the actual admission-to-installation producers. The ordinary internal and forked carriers prove that an unresolved higher-LOCAL_PREF input cannot displace a resolved zero-cost alternate, that loss of both covers removes the retained subject from BGP best, Loc-RIB and every Linux routing table, and that a direct BGP control survives. Restoration uses retained input without another UPDATE. The prospective carriers first install three genuine BGP seeds, establish ordinary resolution of each replacement's next hop, then require withdrawal and absence of the direct and indirectly self-capturing subjects while preserving exact received attributes. The longer interior host route independently resolves the protected replacement, which must remain selected and installed alongside the direct control. Both prospective carriers completed 80 distinct passing runs with all 1120 verification steps each. Read all six accepted 3.1-2 clean/fault receipts and the current native freshness report: all six covers are fresh, with no stale or unproven cover. The ordinary admission-bypass faults fail the initial subject election at 150/24 via unresolved 10.250.1.1; the current-only prospective faults fail the first direct 152/24 forbidden best publication; the over-rejecting containment faults lose protected 154/24 while 151/24 remains visible. The early best-publication failures also violate this Loc-RIB requirement by source coupling: checkRouteBestChange mirrors the selected path before its caller publishes the best change; internal insertion is synchronous, and forked insertion flushes RouteInstall through applyRouteInstall into engine Loc-RIB before returning. With the actual default import configuration, Path.Valid and admit do not add a reachability gate, and upsert retains the selected BGP contribution. FIB rejection cannot repair that Loc-RIB admission. The forked diagnostics show downstream unreachable-route installation before the intended observer failure; route-remove/connection warnings follow failure during shutdown and are not substituted for the semantic failure. This inference is bounded to successful ordinary installation transport and the tested default configuration, not arbitrary RPC failure or rejecting import policy. No direct later Loc-RIB/kernel assertion failure, separately broken indirect case, or separately broken loss/restoration outcome is claimed: fatal primary assertions stop those broken runs. The clean lifecycle assertions and exact protected/direct controls remain mandatory. The bound helper and coupling inventory prevents a later observer, ordering or admission change from retaining this assurance unnoticed.

RFC4271-5.1.2-3

When a given BGP speaker advertises the route to an external peer, the advertising speaker updates the AS_PATH attribute as follows: 1) if the first path segment of the AS_PATH is of type AS_SEQUENCE, the local system prepends its own AS number as the last element of the sequence (put it in the leftmost position with respect to the position of octets in the protocol message). If the act of prepending will cause an overflow in the AS_PATH segment (i.e., more than 255 ASes), it SHOULD prepend a new segment of type AS_SEQUENCE and prepend its own AS number to this new segment. 2) if the first path segment of the AS_PATH is of type AS_SET, the local system prepends a new path segment of type AS_SEQUENCE to the AS_PATH, including its own AS number in that segment. 3) if the AS_PATH is empty, the local system creates a path segment of type AS_SEQUENCE, places its own AS into that segment, and places that segment into the AS_PATH. (§5.1.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_rfc.go::checkRelayWithdrawalShape, internal/le/interoplab/bgp/check_rfc.go::checkRelayWithdrawalShape#2 moved. Independent source rejudgment against committed source b4215935acd576856c4ddfb9160d1d67a6860fd8 after concurrent commit cd0b42213c added TestForwardWithdrawalDoesNotCreateASPath and its native discrimination record. No unrelated working-tree checker edits were included. RFC 4271 Section 5.1.2(b): "When a given BGP speaker advertises the route to an external peer, the advertising speaker updates the AS_PATH attribute as follows:" The following three cases require a local-AS prepend to a leading AS_SEQUENCE, a new leading AS_SEQUENCE before AS_SET, or creation of an AS_SEQUENCE for an empty path, with a new segment recommended on overflow. Section 4.3 separately states: "An UPDATE message might advertise only routes that are to be withdrawn from service, in which case the message will not include path attributes or Network Layer Reachability Information." The existing positive tests cover the actual established announce path with an explicit leading sequence; the ASPathEdit leading-AS_SET and full-255-member sequence branches; and present zero-length AS_PATH through cached and route-server forwarding at both negotiated ASN widths. TestForwardPresentEmptyASPath requires exactly one output, unchanged NLRI and the exact one-member local-AS sequence for an ordinary external recipient, with internal/route-server controls and received-input immutability. TestASPathSlotPrependOnlyWhenAdvertising uses the same prepend intent for advertisement and withdrawal, requires the precise prepended AS-number sequence for the former and no AS_PATH operation for attribute-free withdrawal or MP_UNREACH-only input. The established iBGP control keeps the decoded AS-number list unchanged. Existing limits remain: that iBGP control compares decoded numbers rather than exact TLV bytes, and the overflow test checks the new local-AS segment and retained-segment member count rather than every retained member value. The unchanged checkRelayWithdrawalShape adds independent FRR decoded-path, withdrawal-decode, no-attribute-error and live-session observations, with prior route arrival preceding absence; no fresh interop run is claimed here. The added cd0b42213c test strengthens the negative at actual ForwardUpdate dispatch: forwardOneBody captures exactly one rebuilt raw body from one selected destination; the withdrawal subtest requires absent AS_PATH, complete byte equality and unchanged source input. Its paired advertisement control compares the complete expected UPDATE body, including local AS65000 before source AS65001, next-hop-self192.0.2.1 and unchanged NLRI, and independently checks source immutability. A blanket disabled prepend or next-hop rewrite therefore cannot satisfy that control. The test inspects the dispatched body; this judgment does not extend it into an additional socket-delivery claim. Producing paths were read: ASPathEdit.Record sends non-advertising input to recordWithdrawOnly; recordPrepend/tryShift and ASPath.Prepend implement the sequence, set and empty-path rewrites; forward_build.planAttr independently refuses creation of an absent attribute when advertiseGate is false. The new paired mutation names exactly the two guards that would otherwise mask each other, while each standalone guard can protect the withdrawal. cd0b42213c changes no production source or previous assertion: it adds the bounded negative carrier and proof metadata. The row remains enforced with the stated existing limits. This is source rejudgment only; no new test, guest, paired-mutation, solo-mutation or interop observation is claimed.

RFC4271-9.1.2.2-3

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c13 judge). The MISSING clause (b) of Section 9.1.2.2 (c) is closed at the producer: firstASInPath answers 0 for a path that begins with an AS_SET (quote above the statement), and neighborAS turns 0 into the local AS for an IBGP route (PeerASN == LocalASN). Positive TestRFC4271IBGPAggregatesLedByASSetsCompareMED: two IBGP aggregates led by different AS_SETs share neighbor AS = local AS, MED 10 beats MED 20 at BestStepMED against IGP cost 50 vs 5, and SelectBest agrees. Negative TestRFC4271IBGPRouteLedByASSequenceKeepsItsNeighborAS: an AS_SEQUENCE-led IBGP route keeps neighbor AS 65010 against an aggregate led by AS_SET {65010, 65020}, MED is skipped and the IGP cost decides, so the local-AS rule is not over-applied. HEAD units unchanged: empty-AS_PATH IBGP positive and the later-steps negative (2026-09-30 judge mutants). Side effect read: an EBGP route led by an AS_SET now compares no MED (route-selection.md); such a path breaks Section 5.1.2 b) at its sender, and MED is owed only between routes of one known neighbor AS, so not comparing it stays conformant. Residual outside this row's RFC 4271 reading: the RIB classifies IBGP by PeerASN == LocalASN, not the reactor's RFC 7705-aware rule (journal helper-bypassed-by-an-open-coded-copy.md).

Ze is the BGP speaker this binds once it aggregates. Its oversized opaque-attribute case requires an error and zero callbacks; the fitting control preserves attributes and NLRI in one output. The genuine relay pair supplies protocol-shaped input: a single route with 900 communities fits and is relayed byte-identically; 1100 communities exceed the 4096-byte destination limit and yield ok=false with neither raw nor parsed output. Both forwarding callers explicitly release and skip failed items, so failure is not later dispatched. Final ordinary-write admission also refuses an indivisible effective chunk. The corrected test tag accurately says an error, not an assertion of the exact ErrAttributesTooLarge identity. Logging is optional. Whole-producer halt records are reachability evidence, not selective size-check mutations.

PolarityTestKind and tierTest status

positive TestRFC4271OversizeSingleRouteNotAdvertised unit/verify revert, verified
positive TestRFC4271RelayedRouteTooLargeForThePeerIsWithheld unit/verify revert, verified

RFC4271-9.1.2-5

If the AS_PATH attribute of a BGP route contains an AS loop, the BGP route should be excluded from the Phase 2 decision function (§9.1.2). Detection scans the full AS path and checks that the local autonomous system number does not appear in it. RFC 4271 writes this keyword in lower case, so the level is a recommendation and not a capitalized RFC 2119 SHOULD. The same paragraph places a speaker configured to accept routes with its own autonomous system number in the AS path outside the scope of the document. That out-of-scope case is what the allow-own-as setting selects, so a non-zero allow-own-as is not a deviation from this line.

Audit verdict: not reviewed: no reviewer has checked whether these tests enforce the requirement

positive TestDetectASLoop_NotPresent unit/verify not verified

RFC4271-4.3-7

A BGP speaker SHOULD treat an UPDATE message of this form as though the WITHDRAWN ROUTES do not contain the address prefix. (§4.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_mixed_update_test.go::TestRIBInjectSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_mixed_update_test.go::TestRIBPoolPathSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_mixed_update_test.go::TestRIBSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute moved. Section 4.3 says a mixed UPDATE should be treated as though WITHDRAWN ROUTES did not contain the duplicated prefix. The three existing positives remain: structured receive, JSON/pool receive and injection keep that prefix in their Adj-RIB-In; the structured positive additionally requires the exact BestChangeAdd, no withdrawal, and the same outcome on a repeat UPDATE. Its setup-only repair adds a real connected 10.0.0.1/32 without changing either mixed wire message or assertions. The receive negative names 10/8 and 11/8 withdrawn but reannounces only 10/8, then requires 10/8 present and 11/8 absent from both views, excluding an implementation that ignores the entire withdrawal field. Independent rejudgment preserves the existing verdict, requirement quote, level and full tagged scope. Main receive baseline 4f70ab4b passed all 11 roots (12 leaf cases), race enabled, without skips; supplementary baseline d3723dd3 passed TestLocRIBMirror and TestRIBSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute. Main native refresh 32c5b989 completed all 13 existing receive-cover records: each named clean baseline passed and its existing revert route disabled the named producer with an intentional panic, producing the recorded red. Those observations retain their original producer-disable route and provenance; they are not normal-return semantic-mutant proof and do not add credit to unrecorded supplementary carriers.

RFC4271-6.3-5

If any recognized attribute has Attribute Flags that conflict with the Attribute Type Code, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Attribute Flags Error. The Data field MUST contain the erroneous attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606 3(c) to treat-as-withdraw unless the attribute's specification mandates other flag handling. Flag conflicts are decided by one table (AttributeCode.FlagsConflict via validateAttributeFlags). Tagged units now cover ORIGIN (well-known), MED, AGGREGATOR and ATOMIC_AGGREGATE with conflicting Optional/Transitive bits: exact withdraw-only payload, session Established; the same attributes with specified flags keep the prefix and the value byte for byte. AGGREGATOR flags go to withdraw because RFC 7606 7.7 mandates attribute discard only for its length conditions, not for flags. Observed-red records on validateAttributeFlags both polarities.

RFC4271-6.3-6

If any recognized attribute has an Attribute Length that conflicts with the expected length (based on the attribute type code), then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Attribute Length Error. The Data field MUST contain the erroneous attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606 to per-attribute outcomes. Every attribute whose length its type code fixes now has a length case against its expected length: ORIGIN (withdraw, 7.1), NEXT_HOP (withdraw, 7.3) and MED in TestSessionRFC4271RevisedAttributeErrors, ATOMIC_AGGREGATE and AGGREGATOR (attribute discard, prefix kept, 7.6/7.7) in TestRFC4271RecognizedAttributeErrorsBeyondTheFirst, and LOCAL_PREF in TestRFC4271LocalPrefLengthFromInternalPeer: an iBGP session over the real receive path, length 3 yields exactly the withdrawal of the prefix with the session still Established (7.5), length 4 before and after keeps the prefix with LOCAL_PREF byte-identical. The LOCAL_PREF buffer is otherwise valid, so only the length rule can fire. Observed-red records on validateLocalPrefAttr, validateAggregatorAttr and validateNextHopAttr, both polarities.

RFC4271-6.3-11

The IP address in the NEXT_HOP MUST meet the following criteria to be considered semantically correct: a) It MUST NOT be the IP address of the receiving speaker. b) In the case of an EBGP, where the sender and receiver are one IP hop away from each other, either the IP address in the NEXT_HOP MUST be the sender's IP address that is used to establish the BGP connection, or the interface associated with the NEXT_HOP IP address MUST share a common subnet with the receiving BGP speaker. (§6.3)

The reaction (ignore, no NOTIFICATION) is RFC4271-6.3-2/6.3-3

RFC4271-6.3-14

the same obligation is already captured under another requirement id		 the discard and the Optional Attribute Error subcode are the consequence half of the same obligation, and the row states them If an error is detected, the attribute MUST be discarded, and the Error Subcode MUST be set to Optional Attribute Error.
6.3:22 duplicate-of
never bound Ze
the same obligation is already captured under another requirement id		 the Data field clause belongs to the Optional Attribute Error obligation, and the row states it The Data field MUST contain the attribute (type, length, and value).
7:1 not-a-requirement
never bound Ze
the sentence states a fact or describes another document, and directs no implementation		 the sentence binds the DESIGN of future BGP versions, not the behavior of a BGP-4 implementation: it tells whoever specifies BGP-5 to keep the OPEN and NOTIFICATION formats. No code ze runs can conform to it or violate it, because the obligation is discharged by a future document rather than by a speaker on the wire In order to support BGP version negotiation, future versions of BGP MUST retain the format of the OPEN and NOTIFICATION messages.
9:2 duplicate-of
never bound Ze
the same obligation is already captured under another requirement id		 running the Decision Process is the second half of the withdrawal obligation, and the row states both halves This BGP speaker SHALL run its Decision Process because the previously advertised route is no longer available for use.
9.1.4:3 duplicate-of
never bound Ze
the same obligation is already captured under another requirement id		 the next sentence defines the act: 'That is, the NLRI of this route cannot be more specific.' That is the Section 5.1.6 prohibition on making the NLRI of an ATOMIC_AGGREGATE route more specific, restated where overlapping routes are discussed In particular, a route that carries the ATOMIC_AGGREGATE attribute MUST NOT be de-aggregated.

9.2.2.2:6 feature-out-of-scope
never bound Ze
the RFC makes a feature OPTIONAL, Ze decided not to offer it, and this obligation is conditional on offering it		 an AS_SEQUENCE condition on an aggregated AS_PATH ze never builds, because ze performs no route aggregation. The RFC makes the choice the speaker's: "If a BGP speaker chooses to aggregate, then it SHOULD either include" - all tuples of type AS_SEQUENCE in the aggregated AS_PATH SHALL appear in all of the AS_PATHs in the initial set of routes to be aggregated.
9.2.2.2:7 feature-out-of-scope
never bound Ze
the RFC makes a feature OPTIONAL, Ze decided not to offer it, and this obligation is conditional on offering it		 an AS_SET condition on an aggregated AS_PATH ze never builds, because ze performs no route aggregation. The RFC makes the choice the speaker's: "If a BGP speaker chooses to aggregate, then it SHOULD either include" - all tuples of type AS_SET in the aggregated AS_PATH SHALL appear in at least one of the AS_PATHs in the initial set (they may appear as either AS_SET or AS_SEQUENCE types).
9.2.2.2:8 feature-out-of-scope
never bound Ze
the RFC makes a feature OPTIONAL, Ze decided not to offer it, and this obligation is conditional on offering it		 a duplicate-AS_SET condition on an aggregated AS_PATH ze never builds, because ze performs no route aggregation. The RFC makes the choice the speaker's: "If a BGP speaker chooses to aggregate, then it SHOULD either include" - No tuple of type AS_SET with the same value SHALL appear more than once in the aggregated AS_PATH.

E:1 duplicate-of
never bound Ze
the same obligation is already captured under another requirement id		 Appendix E restates the Security Considerations obligation to support the TCP MD5 option of RFC 2385 An implementation MUST support the TCP MD5 option [RFC2385].
F.6:1 duplicate-of
never bound Ze
the same obligation is already captured under another requirement id		 Appendix F.6 restates the same RFC 2385 authentication obligation as a change from RFC 1771 A BGP implementation MUST support the authentication mechanism specified in RFC 2385 [RFC2385].

Superseded

No document obsoletes RFC 4271, so its obligations are stated where they were written.
